# Proposed OEIS comments: A289763 to A297983

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A289763

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A289763>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3716](../papers/03501-04000/03716-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> G.f.: (1 - 10*x + 100*x^2 - 1000*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 10*x)*(1 - 10*x^2)*(1 + 100*x^2)) (conjectured). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 14 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A294159

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A294159>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9843](../papers/09501-10000/09843-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: D-finite with recurrence n*a(n) +(-3*n+1)*a(n-1) +2*(-n+3)*a(n-2) +2*(n-5)*a(n-3) +(n-4)*a(n-4) +(n-5)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 17 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A295036

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A295036>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [5352](../papers/05001-05500/05352-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) +11*a(n-2) +110*a(n-3) +218*a(n-4) +381*a(n-5) -1782*a(n-6) -2295*a(n-7) +4944*a(n-8) +5724*a(n-9) -6097*a(n-10) -7938*a(n-11) -1252*a(n-12) +14108*a(n-13) +11254*a(n-14) -22229*a(n-15) -16840*a(n-16) +27235*a(n-17) -25020*a(n-18) +11308*a(n-19) +6762*a(n-20) -1639*a(n-21) +2585*a(n-22) +642*a(n-23) -2303*a(n-24) -429*a(n-25) +661*a(n-26) -215*a(n-27) +117*a(n-28) -14*a(n-29) -36*a(n-30) +7*a(n-31) +4*a(n-32) +2*a(n-33) -a(n-34)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 35. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A295037

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A295037>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [9999](../papers/09501-10000/09999-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 91 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 92 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 91, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```


Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

```

Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 240 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. ```
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) +45*a(n-2) +198*a(n-3) +1120*a(n-4) -571*a(n-5) +949*a(n-6) -25951*a(n-7) +43907*a(n-8) -121981*a(n-9) +237707*a(n-10) -305033*a(n-11) +951497*a(n-12) -2179565*a(n-13) +1393985*a(n-14) +38155*a(n-15) +856624*a(n-16) +1417955*a(n-17) -2925138*a(n-18) +4386425*a(n-19) -3349236*a(n-20) +7904975*a(n-21) -936917*a(n-22) +6331851*a(n-23) -431305*a(n-24) -2123285*a(n-25) -1887476*a(n-26) -6000225*a(n-27) -5171754*a(n-28) -5025966*a(n-29) -3580724*a(n-30) +728778*a(n-31) +1200558*a(n-32) +668712*a(n-33) +511075*a(n-34) +1377776*a(n-35) +458144*a(n-36) +151448*a(n-37) -54584*a(n-38) -42912*a(n-39) -30464*a(n-40) +3840*a(n-41)

## A297698

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A297698>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10329](../papers/10001-10500/10329-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 60 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 83 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 60, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
